# Mistakes found in published sources

The atlas's workers read papers and books line by line to extract and plan their mathematics, and they record every mistake they find in them: misprints (slips whose intended meaning is clear), errors (false statements or steps that fail) and gaps (steps asserted without an adequate proof). A finding counts as confirmed once an independent reviewer has checked it at its locator. Each finding says where its worker looked for an existing correction; a new one is not corrected in any erratum or later version they found. Nothing here has been sent to the authors.

Collected by `scripts/errata.py` from the paper extractions and blueprint packets (research/blueprint/PROTOCOL.md, section 18); the data is in `data/source-issues.json`.

0 new mistakes confirmed · 248 awaiting review · 34 already corrected in print · 0 rejected on review · 10 extractions and packets not yet checked.

None yet.

## New mistakes awaiting review

### Bakker–Klingler–Tsimerman, "Tame topology of arithmetic quotients and algebraicity of Hodge loci", Journal of the American Mathematical Society (2020) (`PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20`)

- **Error** at Theorem 4.1, p.13 of the author copy (= arXiv 1810.04801v2, p.13); the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Theorem 4.1. Let V be a polarized variation of pure Hodge structures of weight k over the punctured polydisk (∆*)^n, with period map Φ: (∆*)^n → S_{Γ,G,M}. Then Φ is R_an,exp-definable." (∆ is the unit disk, p.4.)`; it should be `Definability holds only after shrinking. For some radius 0 < ρ < 1, the restriction of Φ to (∆*_ρ)^n is R_an,exp-definable, and then for every ρ < 1 by compactness of the remaining annular pieces. The reduction in §4.1 must use charts whose shrunken polydisks still cover the compactification.`. Counterexample (n = 1, trivial monodromy). Let f(z) = exp(−(1+z)/(1−z)) on the unit disk, so |f| < 1, and τ = 2i + εf with ε small, so that τ(∆) lies in a disk on which π: H → Γ\H is injective (Γ torsion-free, e.g. Γ(3)). τ is the period map of a polarized weight-one integral VHS. But Φ^{−1}(π(2i + ε/e)) contains the points z_n = 2πin/(2 + 2πin), n ≠ 0, where f = 1/e. These form an infinite discrete set accumulating at 1, which is impossible for a fibre of a definable map. The proof itself allows for this: it says 'after maybe shrinking the polydisk' (p.14), and its Siegel set S_H (y > 1) covers only |q| < e^{−2π}. Theorem 1.3 is unaffected, since §4.1 can use finitely many shrunken charts. Checked numerically: |f(z_n) − 1/e| < 1e−8 for 0 < |n| ≤ 2000. Noted by the extraction (item local-outer-boundary-obstruction). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).
- **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 4.5, p.15 of the author copy (= arXiv v2 p.15); the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1) (it affects the proof). The source says `"Consider for each positive integer m and real number c the function f_{m,c} which we obtain from f by setting z_1 = mz_2 + c. Note that the assumptions of the lemma still apply to f_{m,c}, and it follows by induction on n that f_{m,c} ≪ 1 on all of Σ_{n−1}"`; it should be `State the lemma, and verify its curve hypothesis, on the wider sectors Σ_n^R = {|x_i| < R, y_1 ≥ ⋯ ≥ y_n > 1} for every R, with constants depending on R. The substitution then maps Σ_{n−1}^{R′} into Σ_n^{mR′+c}, and the induction goes through unchanged. In the application in §4.5, the curve bounds come from Schmid's one-variable Corollary 5.29 and the norm estimates of Theorem 4.8, which hold for real parts in any bounded interval; Theorem 1.5 is already stated on H^n_{R,η} for all R.`. Σ_n is defined with 0 < x_i < 1 (Definition 4.4). The proof uses c = 0, 1, …, r_1 and m ≥ 1. For c ≥ 1, x_1 = mx_2 + c > 1, so Σ_n ∩ {z_1 = mz_2 + c} is empty. For c = 0 it is only the strip x_2 < 1/m. So the hypothesis gives no information about f_{m,c} on Σ_{n−1}. The functions extend there (the coefficients in O are periodic in x and f is polynomial in x), but the bound is assumed only on Σ_n. With the R-uniform hypothesis, the finite difference in c isolates the top x_1-degree, the Vandermonde step in m isolates each y-power, and |x_1| < 1 on Σ_n finishes the proof, as printed. Noted by the extraction (verification gap G4); the repair is given by this continuation. Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).
- **Error** at Proof of Theorem 1.5 (§4.5), p.18 of the author copy; the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1). The source says `"Moreover, if b is (e′, C′)-reduced and e is a basis for which condition (3) of Definition 4.11 holds for some C > 0, then b will also be (e, C″)-reduced for some C″ = C″(e, C, e′, C′) > 0."`; it should be `Condition (2), the ordering b(e_i) < C b(e_j) for i < j, transfers only up to a permutation of e. Conclude that b is (σe, C″)-reduced for some permutation σ, which is one of finitely many.`. Take e′ = (e_1, e_2), e = (e_2, e_1) and b = diag(1, T) with T ≥ 1. Then b is (e′, 2)-reduced and satisfies condition (3) for e (b(e_2)b(e_1) = T = det b), but condition (2) for e would need T = b(e_2) < C″b(e_1) = C″ for all T. The proof immediately adds 'we may assume (2) as there are only finitely many orderings of the basis', which is the corrected form, so Theorem 1.5 is unaffected. Noted by the extraction (item basis-change-reducedness). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E3`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).
- **Misprint** at Appendix A.1, p.20 of the author copy; the JAMS typeset version was not collated (publisher PDF not obtainable here, verification gap G1). The source says `"Given X a real-analytic n-manifold with corners, one defines its boundary ∂X (cf. [Joy12, def. 2.6]. This is a real-analytic n-manifold with corners for n > 0"`; it should be `∂X is a real-analytic (n − 1)-manifold with corners.`. The boundary of an n-manifold with corners in Joyce's sense is (n − 1)-dimensional; for [0,∞) it is a point. The appendix itself later says 'the compact real-analytic (n − 1)-manifold with corners ∂X' (proof of Proposition A.2). Noted by the extraction (item corner-boundary). Recorded as `PAPER-BAKKER-KLINGLER-TSIMERMAN-20/E4`; looked for an existing correction in: The official erratum (JAMS 36 (2023), DOI 10.1090/jams/1025), read in full: it corrects only the maximal-compact dependence, morphisms and the Siegel-set pullback citation; arXiv 1810.04801 v2 (2020-06-22, the latest version): same wording at each locator; Benjamin Bakker's web page (author copy and erratum).

### Bakker–Tsimerman, "p-torsion monodromy representations of elliptic curves over geometric function fields", Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), no. 3 (`PAPER-BAKKER-TSIMERMAN-16`)

- **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 23, p.735, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 709–744 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 814976ee…f40a). The source says `"dd^c(f∘χ) = −(1/2π) dd^c log(|1 − z w̄|² − |z − w|²) + dd^c log|z − w|² = −(1/2π) dd^c log[(1 − |z|²)(1 − |w|²)] + [Δ_D] = (1/4π) ω_{D×D} + [Δ_D]."`; it should be `The coefficient in the first two lines is −1: f∘χ = log|z − w|² − log(|1 − z w̄|² − |z − w|²), so dd^c(f∘χ) = −dd^c log[(1 − |z|²)(1 − |w|²)] + [Δ_D] = (1/4π) ω_{D×D} + [Δ_D].`. With f(s) = log(s/(1 − s)) and χ = |z − w|²/|1 − z w̄|², f∘χ is the stated difference of logarithms with coefficient 1; the final line is consistent with the paper's d^c normalization only with coefficient −1 in front of the first term. Found by the review REV-PAPER-BAKKER-TSIMERMAN-16 (item 117). - **Misprint** at Proof of Theorem 29, p.742, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 709–744 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 814976ee…f40a). The source says `"Thus, Ram(α) = o(Deg C), and Riemann–Hurwitz applied to α yields 2g(C) − 2 = o(Deg C)."`; it should be `Riemann–Hurwitz for α : C → P¹ gives 2g(C) − 2 = −2 deg α + Ram(α) ≤ o(Deg C). The inequality is what the contradiction with 2g(C) − 2 ≫ Deg(C) needs.`. The printed equality drops the term −2 deg α, which is not o(Deg C) in general. Found by the extraction (item 90). - **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 4.1, p.3, in the authors' erratum (B. Bakker and J. Tsimerman, Erratum, 5 pp., https://benjamin-bakker.github.io/P.torsion.erratum.pdf, SHA-256 db8effa1…acc1). The source says `"mult_{CM−∩Δ̄}(T*_m C) ≪ … ≪ vol(T*_m C ∩ B(Δ̄, ε)) ≪ (sinh(ρ_{X(p)}/2)/sinh(ε)) vol(T*_m C)"`; it should be `The factor is sinh(ε)/sinh(ρ_{X(p)}/2): Proposition 3.1 gives vol(C ∩ B(Δ̄,ε)) ≤ (sinh ε/sinh R) vol(C ∩ B(Δ̄,R)) for 0 < ε < R < ρ/2, and letting R ↑ ρ_{X(p)}/2 gives the small factor.`. Proposition 3.1 says (1/sinh r)·vol(C ∩ B(Δ̄,r)) increases with r, so the small ball has at most the proportion sinh ε/sinh R of the large one. The printed reciprocal is a large factor (ρ_{X(p)} ~ 2 log p) and would not give the p^{−1} saving the next line states, (p^{−2δ₂} + d₃p^{−1}) Deg(C). - **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 15, pp.726–727, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 709–744 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 814976ee…f40a). The source says `"Since M′² = −1, it follows that M′ is either equal to t̃ or −t̃" … "Thus g = a + b(0 1; −1 0), … and (x,y) lies on T_m where m = det g = a² + b²."`; it should be `Treat the order-three case separately: there t̃ satisfies t̃² − t̃ + 1 = 0 (H ∩ M₂(Z) ≅ Z[e^{2πi/3}]), so M′ satisfies the same Eisenstein relation and M′ ∈ {t̃, 1 − t̃}, and g = a + b·t₀ with m = det g = a² + ab + b². - **Error** at §2.5, Hecke operators, p.717, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 709–744 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 814976ee…f40a). The source says `T_n is defined (p.716) as the closure of pairs related by a cyclic isogeny of degree n, and then: "The two projection maps α, β : T_n → X(p) both have degree deg(T_n) = σ₁(n) = Σ_{d|n} d".`; it should be `For the cyclic correspondence deg(T_n) = ψ(n) = n∏_{ℓ|n}(1 + 1/ℓ); σ₁(n) is the degree of the full correspondence over all subgroups of order n.`. For n = 4 there are six cyclic subgroups of order 4 but σ₁(4) = 7 (the extra subgroup is E[2]). The later estimates use only a polynomial bound such as deg(T_m) = O(m²), which both satisfy. ### Bennett–Siksek, "A conjecture of Erdős, supersingular primes and short character sums", Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), no. 2 (`PAPER-BENNETT-SIKSEK-20`)

- **Error** at Lemma 3.2, p.361, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 355–392 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3920a752…). The source says `"Its discriminant is Δ_a = 64(a_a b_a c_a)² = (2⁸/g⁶)(n+id)²(n+jd)²(n+(2j−i)d)²."`; it should be `Δ_a = 16(a_a b_a c_a)² = (2⁶/g⁶)(n+id)²(n+jd)²(n+(2j−i)d)².`. For y² = x³ + a₂x² + a₄x, Δ = 16a₄²(a₂² − 4a₄). For E_a: Y² = X(X−a)(X+c), a₂ = c − a and a₄ = −ac, so Δ = 16a²c²(a+c)² = 16(abc)² since a+b+c = 0; with b_a = −2(n+jd)/g this is 2⁶/g⁶ times the product. The extraction found this (item 16, report D1); the review re-derived it. Recorded as `PAPER-BENNETT-SIKSEK-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2020/191-2/p02, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or corrigendum is linked; arXiv 1709.01022: only v1 (4 September 2017) exists, which predates the published text; no later version; web search for an erratum, corrigendum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found; S. Siksek's homepage (samirsiksek.github.io) lists the paper with no correction; M. A. Bennett's homepage and CV page (personal.math.ubc.ca/~bennett) list no correction.
- **Error** at Theorem 5, equation (26), p.374, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 355–392 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3920a752…). The source says `"Let χ be a primitive Dirichlet character of conductor N. Then Σ_{m≤X} χ(m)Λ(m) = δ_χX − X^{β_χ}/β_χ + O(X exp(−c log X/(√(log X) + log N))·(log N)⁴)."`; it should be `State it for N > 1, or replace (log N)⁴ by (log 2N)⁴; for the principal character (N = 1) the printed error term is 0.`. With N = 1 the formula would assert ψ(X) = X exactly. The paper uses only nonprincipal characters with N > 1. The extraction found this (item 89, report D13). - **Gap** at Theorem 6, pp.376–377, and Case 1 of the proof of Proposition 8.2, pp.377–378, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 355–392 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3920a752…) (it affects the proof). The source says `"Let π_i be characters of conductor q_i for 1 ≤ i ≤ r", applied with "ψ … principal of conductor M₂" and a factorization with "(d) 1 < q_r ≤ k^{7/16}".`; it should be `The later factors are characters of modulus q_i (a principal character has conductor 1). When M₂ = 1 there is no principal factor, so the case must be handled separately and modulus-one factors discarded; a packing with at most ⌈10c₂⌉ + 4 factors suffices.`. - **Gap** at §4, proof of Lemma 4.1, second case, p.364, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 355–392 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3920a752…). The source says `"From (2) and (12), we have p | A, ℓ | ord_p(A) and p ∤ B. Equation (13) thus implies that p ∤ κ and so the model E_i has multiplicative reduction at p."`; it should be `Here p ∈ (k/2, k], outside the range p ≥ k of Lemma 3.4. Justify minimality directly: c₄(E_i) = 16κ(4κd² − 3A) is a p-unit because p | A and p ∤ κd, so E_i is minimal with multiplicative reduction at p and ℓ | ord_p(Δ_i).`. Lemma 3.4 gives minimality and semistability only at primes p ≥ k, and the text gives no argument for p < k. The conclusion is true by the c₄ computation. - **Misprint** at §9, proof of Proposition 9.1, p.384, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 191 (2020), 355–392 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3920a752…). The source says `"Since no prime p ≥ k^{7/16} divides any of these A_i, Stirling's formula …"`; it should be `"Since no prime p > k^{7/16} divides any of these A_i …"`. The sieve removes only the primes in (k^{7/16}, k]; the weak inequality is what the subsequent product over p > k^{7/16} uses. The extraction found this (item 106, report D12). ### Benoist–Wittenberg, "On the integral Hodge conjecture for real varieties, I", Inventiones Mathematicae (2020) (`PAPER-BENOIST-WITTENBERG-20`)

- **Error** at Theorem 3.16(ii), p.55, and Theorem A(ii), p.4, in the published version, Inventiones mathematicae 222 (2020), 1–77 (author-hosted copy with journal pagination, SHA-256 daeb43ec…46a9) (it affects a stated result). The source says `Theorem A: "Let X be a smooth, proper and geometrically irreducible real variety, of dimension d ≥ 1. Assume that X satisfies the real integral Hodge conjecture for 1-cycles and that H²(X, O_X) = 0. … (ii) There exists a geometrically irreducible curve of even geometric genus in X if and only if the natural map Pic(X)[2^∞] → Pic(X_C)^G[2^∞] is onto." Theorem 3.16 assumes "dim(X) > 0".`; it should be `Add the hypothesis d ≥ 2 (or X(R) = ∅); the proof, which goes through Proposition 3.9, needs d ≥ 2 when X(R) ≠ ∅.`. Counterexample in dimension one: X = E, the real elliptic curve y² = x³ − x. Here H²(E, O_E) = 0 and the Hodge conjecture for 1-cycles (codimension 0) holds. The only geometrically irreducible curve in E is E itself, of geometric genus 1, so the left side fails. Since E(R) ≠ ∅, Br(R) → Br(E) is injective and Pic(E) → Pic(E_C)^G is an isomorphism, so the right side holds. Proposition 3.9 (a curve of odd geometric genus exists) is stated for dimension ≥ 2, as the introduction says (p.4). The surface and higher-dimensional cases, which are the paper's applications, are unaffected. Found by the extraction (items printed-even-genus, main-even-genus). Recorded as `PAPER-BENOIST-WITTENBERG-20/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: O. Wittenberg's publications page https://www.math.univ-paris13.fr/~wittenberg/ lists this paper and its sequel (part II, J. Éc. polytech. Math. 7 (2020)) with no erratum for part I, while it does list an erratum for another Benoist–Wittenberg paper (checked 22 September 2026); arXiv 1801.00872: v1 (2018), v2 (2019), v3 (11 March 2020); v3 has the same Theorem 3.16; web search for an erratum or corrigendum to part I, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Misprint** at §2.2, paragraph before Example 2.5, p.37, in the published version, Inventiones mathematicae 222 (2020), 1–77 (author-hosted copy with journal pagination, SHA-256 daeb43ec…46a9). The source says `"Namely, let X be a smooth and proper variety over a real closed field R and let k ≥ 0 be even, so that Z(2k) = Z(k). We can view ω^{2k} … as an element of H^{2k}_G(X(C), Z(k)). This class is Hodge if R = R, being torsion, and it belongs to H^{2k}_G(X(C), Z(k))_0 if (and only if) X(R) = ∅."`; it should be `"let k > 0 be even".`. For k = 0, ω^0 = 1 is not torsion, and it is the class of X itself, hence algebraic and in the subgroup cut out by the topological condition even when X(R) ≠ ∅; the "if and only if" fails. For even k > 0 the statement is as printed. Noted by the extraction (item constant-omega). Recorded as `PAPER-BENOIST-WITTENBERG-20/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: O. Wittenberg's publications page https://www.math.univ-paris13.fr/~wittenberg/ lists this paper and its sequel (part II, J. Éc. polytech. Math. 7 (2020)) with no erratum for part I, while it does list an erratum for another Benoist–Wittenberg paper (checked 22 September 2026); arXiv 1801.00872: v1 (2018), v2 (2019), v3 (11 March 2020); v3 has the same Theorem 3.16; web search for an erratum or corrigendum to part I, 22 September 2026: none found.

### Bhatt et al., "Globally +-regular varieties and the minimal model program for threefolds in mixed characteristic", Publications Mathématiques de l'IHÉS (2023) (`PAPER-BHATT-ETAL-23`)

- **Error** at Lemma 2.56, second assertion, p.101 of the published version; the same wording is arXiv v1 Lemma 2.37 (p.20), v2 Lemma 2.50 (p.26) and v3 Lemma 2.56 (p.29) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"More generally, let D be a fixed divisor and let A be a semiample Q-Cartier Q-divisor such that A ∼_Q M + Λ, where M is an effective Cartier divisor with no exceptional curve of Y → X in its support, and −δD ≤ Λ ≤ δD for δ > 0. Take x ∈ F ∩ M of multiplicity k ∈ Z_{>0} in M. Then ε_sa(A; x) converges to k when δ → 0."`; it should be `Only a lower bound holds. For every rational γ with 0 < γ < k there is δ₀ > 0, depending only on f, D and γ, such that ε_sa(A; x) = ε(A; x) ≥ k − γ for all δ < δ₀ and all such A, M, Λ and x. Equivalently, liminf_{δ→0} ε_sa(A; x) ≥ k, uniformly. This is exactly what the printed proof establishes; there is no upper bound.`. Counterexample with Λ = 0. Let k be a field of characteristic p > 0, X = A²_k, and f: Y → X the blow-up of the origin with exceptional curve E = F. Let M be the sum of the strict transforms of the two axes. Then M ∼ −2E, so M is effective, Cartier, globally generated over X (O_Y(−E) = m·O_Y) and has no exceptional component. Let x = E ∩ (strict transform of the y-axis), so mult_x M = 1 = k. Blow up x, with exceptional curve G and strict transform E′. Then E′² = −2, G² = −1, E′·G = 1 and π*M − tG ∼ −2E′ − (2+t)G, which has degree 2 − t on E′ and t on G. Hence ε(M; x) = 2. At t = 2 the ideal (x, y²) pulls back to O(−E′ − 2G) (charts x = ab, y = b, then a = c, b = cd, giving c²d, and a = ef, b = f, giving f²). So π*M − 2G ∼ −2E′ − 4G is globally generated and ε_sa(M; x) = 2. Taking A = M and Λ = 0 (any D), ε_sa(A; x) = 2 for every δ, not converging to k = 1. The proof only shows that π*A − (k − γ)E is nef. The consumers need only the lower bound: Proposition 8.15 (p.173) uses the first assertion ε_sa(M; x) ≥ 1, which is correct, and Claim 8.21 (p.178) needs ε_sa > 2 for k = 3. So the paper's results are unaffected. Checked on the page image and by exact arithmetic. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic A, gap-seshadri-limit); verified and proved in the corrected form by this continuation. - **Misprint** at Proposition 6.28, p.154 of the published version (and arXiv v3 Proposition 6.28, p.71). The source says `"Let Δ ≥ 0 be a Q-divisor on X such that (X, Δ) is globally +-regular (completely purely globally +-regular over R, resp.). Suppose that Δ_Y ≥ 0, where K_Y + Δ_Y = f*(K_X + Δ). Then (Y, Δ_Y) is globally +-regular (globally +-regular, resp.)."`; it should be `The conclusion in the resp. case is "completely purely globally +-regular over R", with the pure boundary S_Y the strict transform of S and B_Y = Δ_Y − S_Y. This is what arXiv v2 (Proposition 6.28, p.64) prints, and arXiv v1 (Proposition 6.22, p.48) prints "purely ... purely".`. The printed resp. conclusion is false. Take R = k[[t]] with k of characteristic p, X = Y = Spec R, f = id and Δ = S = V(t). Then (X, S) is purely globally +-regular. For a finite normal R′ ⊇ R with uniformizer s and ramification e, the map R → s^{1−e}R′ splits because R′ is R-free and 1 ∉ t·s^{1−e}R′ = sR′. But (X, S) is not globally +-regular: already for the identity cover, a splitting of O_X → O_X(S) = t^{−1}R would send 1 = t·t^{−1} into tR. The cited proof (Proposition 6.26, which applies because R is complete, together with Lemma 4.27 with B′ = B_Y) proves the pure conclusion. The consumer in Theorem 7.14 (p.165) uses the pure conclusion. In arXiv v3 the qualifier is visibly garbled as "( ly globally +-regular, resp.)", so the published wording is a typesetting slip. Checked on the page images of v3 p.71 and published p.154. Noted by the extraction (diagnostic B, gap-pure-birational); the version history was established by this continuation. The source says `"Twisting by the pullback L⁺ to W⁺ of the line bundle O_Y(f*(M − (K_X + S + B))) (for some finite cover f: Y → W), and taking local cohomology, we obtain: ... The left zeros are due to Corollary 3.7 and the fact that L⁺ is the pullback of a big and semiample line bundle."`; it should be `Twist by the pullback of O_Y(f*(K_X + S + B − M)), the inverse of the big and semiample bundle f*(M − (K_X + S + B)). Corollary 3.7 then applies with b = −1 and kills H^{d−1}_m of it.`. Corollary 3.7 (p.107) gives H^i(RΓ_x RΓ(X⁺, L^b)) = 0 only for b < 0 and i < dim X, where L is big and semiample, so it gives nothing for the positive twist that is printed. With the inverse twist, H^d_m of O(−S_{W⁺}) twisted by f*(K_X + S + B − M) is dual to H⁰(O_Y(K_Y + S_Y + f*(M − K_X − S − B))), which is the expression in the statement of the lemma. The same line bundle L := O_{X⁺}(π*(K_X + S + B − M)) appears in the proof of Theorem 7.2 (p.156). The lemma is marked "a fact we will not use". Checked on the page image. ### Bresciani, "On the birational section conjecture with strong birationality assumptions", Inventiones Mathematicae (2024) (`PAPER-BRESCIANI-24`)

- **Misprint** at §2, two citations of [Bre21b, Proposition A.18], v3 p.5 (published pp.133 and 135), in arXiv 2108.13397v3 (15 September 2023, the final author version; PDF SHA-256 e23989fb…b722); published page numbers are those the extraction recorded from the open-access publisher PDF (SHA-256 77c20bc7…e148), which could not be re-fetched here. The source says `"Furthermore, the étale fundamental gerbe behaves well with respect to base change in characteristic 0 [Bre21b, Proposition A.18]." and "If k′/k is a field extension and c : Spec k′ → C is a point, the induced morphism Π_{X_c/k′} → Π_{X/C} ×_C Spec k′ is an isomorphism: using the fact that the étale fundamental gerbe behaves well under base change [Bre21b, Proposition A.18] …"`; it should be `[Bre21b, Proposition A.23].`. In the published [Bre21b] (Algebr. Geom. 8 (2021), 231–267, the version cited), Proposition A.18 (p.262) treats only algebraic separable extensions k′/k. Proposition A.23 (p.264) gives Π_{X_{k′}/k′} ≅ Π_{X/k} ×_k k′ for every field extension of a characteristic-0 field and geometrically connected concentrated X. The paper needs arbitrary extensions, above all k(t)/k and residue fields of points of C, so A.23 is the result meant; A.23's own proof starts from A.18. Both statements were read in the published [Bre21b] (SHA-256 768ca87a…2908). Noted by the extraction (item 26). Recorded as `PAPER-BRESCIANI-24/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Inventiones mathematicae: Crossref record of DOI 10.1007/s00222-023-01220-6 checked for updates, 22 September 2026: none registered; arXiv 2108.13397: v1 (30 August 2021), v2 (12 February 2022), v3 (15 September 2023, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found; F. Pop, "Generalizations and minimalistic refinements of the t-birational Section Conjecture", arXiv 2604.26131, was searched for comments on this paper and makes none.
- **Error** at §4, the paragraph after Corollary 15 on parabolic curves, v3 p.11 (published p.141), in arXiv 2108.13397v3 (15 September 2023, the final author version; PDF SHA-256 e23989fb…b722); published page numbers are those the extraction recorded from the open-access publisher PDF (SHA-256 77c20bc7…e148), which could not be re-fetched here (it affects the proof). The source says `"We say that a curve X is parabolic if it has genus 0 and the degree of X̄ ∖ X is at most 2. If X is proper of genus 0, then there is a unique Galois section, and it is geometric if and only if X = P¹. If X is parabolic affine, every Galois section of X is cuspidal (the only non-trivial case is settled by [Sch15, Theorem A]). Because of this, we can always assume that X is non-parabolic."`; it should be `The sentence is false as stated. What the later reductions need is a statement about the t-birationally liftable (or birationally liftable) sections of a parabolic affine X, which have to be treated directly, including the case where X̄ ∖ X is a single point of degree 2. The reductions to non-parabolic X rest on this: "We may assume that X is non-parabolic" in the proof of Lemma 16, and "it is immediate to check that t-b.l. sections are quasi-t-b.l." after Definition 18, where the identity map is not available for parabolic X.`. Counterexample. Let k = Q and X = P¹_Q ∖ V(T² − 2). Then X is affine, of genus 0, with boundary a single point of degree 2, so it is parabolic. The paper defines a Galois section to be cuspidal if it is associated with a k-rational point of X̄ ∖ X (§1). The boundary has no Q-rational point, so X has no cuspidal sections. But 0 ∈ X(Q), so X has a geometric section. Schmidt's theorem concerns the existence of rational points and splitting; it does not make a degree-2 boundary point rational. The paper's Theorems A–C are not claimed to fail here, only this justification of the reduction. Noted by the extraction (item 77, with the reduction gap in items 81, 96 and 112). Recorded as `PAPER-BRESCIANI-24/E2`; looked for an existing correction in: Inventiones mathematicae: Crossref record of DOI 10.1007/s00222-023-01220-6 checked for updates, 22 September 2026: none registered; arXiv 2108.13397: v1 (30 August 2021), v2 (12 February 2022), v3 (15 September 2023, the latest); web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found; F. Pop, "Generalizations and minimalistic refinements of the t-birational Section Conjecture", arXiv 2604.26131, was searched for comments on this paper and makes none.
- **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 26, v3 p.17 (published p.148), in arXiv 2108.13397v3 (15 September 2023, the final author version; PDF SHA-256 e23989fb…b722); published page numbers are those the extraction recorded from the open-access publisher PDF (SHA-256 77c20bc7…e148), which could not be re-fetched here (it affects the proof). The source says `"For every open subset U ⊂ X, denote by ι_U(s) the image of s in S_{U/k}, by hypothesis it is cuspidal. By [Sti12, Theorem 17 (2)], there exists a unique rational point x ∈ X̄(k) associated with ι_X(s) … For every open subset U ⊂ X, denote by P_{U,x} ⊂ S_{U/k} the packet of cuspidal sections associated with x. … Since ι_U(s) ∈ P_{U,x} for every U ⊂ X and s is the limit of the sections ι_U(s), we get that s is cuspidal associated with x too."`; it should be `By hypothesis each ι_U(s) is geometric or cuspidal, not necessarily cuspidal. Once the associated point x ∈ X̄(k) is found, restrict to the cofinal family of opens U with x ∉ U. For those, ι_U(s) is cuspidal at x, and S_{k(X)/k} = lim_U S_{U/k} may be computed along this family, so the conclusion follows.`. The hypothesis of the lemma is that every birationally liftable section is geometric or cuspidal. If x ∈ X, then for opens U containing x the section ι_U(s) is the geometric section at x, which is not in the cuspidal packet P_{U,x}. So "ι_U(s) ∈ P_{U,x} for every U ⊂ X" fails for those U, although the limit argument goes through on the cofinal subfamily. Noted by the extraction (item 127). ### Burungale–Kobayashi–Ota, "Rubin's conjecture on local units in the anticyclotomic tower at inert primes", Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021), no. 3 (`PAPER-BURUNGALE-KOBAYASHI-OTA-21`)

- **Error** at Lemma 5.2(ii), p.956, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 194 (2021), 943–966 (Caltech repository copy of the publisher PDF, SHA-256 77ff3290…790e) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"(ii) The number of supersingular points in X₀(N)(F_{p²}) that correspond to supersingular elliptic curves over F_{p²} with the Frobenius trace a_{p²} = 2p or −2p and the j-invariant different from 0 and 1728 is at least µ(⌊(p−1)/12⌋ − 3/2 + (−3/p) + ½(−1/p)). In particular, there is a supersingular point with a_{p²} = 2p or −2p in X₀(N)(F_{p²}) over X(1)(F_{p²})∖{0,1728} that is unramified for π if p ≡ 1 mod 12 or p ≥ 31."`; it should be `The "in particular" follows for p ≡ 1 mod 12 and for p ≥ 31 with p ≠ 47. At p = 47 the displayed lower bound is 0 and gives nothing. The existence of an unramified supersingular point at p = 47 comes instead from Lemma 5.2(iii), since 47 ≡ 2 mod 3.`. The conclusion needs the lower bound to exceed the number of ramification points, which (i) bounds by µ/6. At p = 47: ⌊46/12⌋ = 3, (−3/47) = −1 (47 ≡ 2 mod 3) and (−1/47) = −1 (47 ≡ 3 mod 4), so the bound is µ(3 − 3/2 − 1 − 1/2) = 0 < µ/6. A computation over all primes 31 ≤ p ≤ 400 finds 47 as the only failure, and beyond that the floor term dominates. The main theorem is unaffected: Lemma 5.2(iii) covers p = 47, and Rubin's own verification (Remark 2.3) covers small p. Found by the review REV-PAPER-BURUNGALE-KOBAYASHI-OTA-21. The source says `"Let ν ∈ X_l be a Hecke character of order l^m. We define ξ_ν as the image of z_{fl^m} …" and "Proposition 3.3. Let ν ∈ X_l be a character of order l^m", while the same construction uses K(fl^m p^{n+1}), "the conductor fl^m of ϕν" (proof of 3.3(3)) and "let l^m be the conductor" (p.954).`; it should be `Read l^m as the conductor of ν throughout §3.0.2–3.0.3 (so that ν factors through K(fl^m) and the elliptic unit z_{fl^m} applies), with the anticyclotomic layer L_m indexed accordingly.`. For l split in K, the l-part of the ring class group of conductor l^n is (Z/l^n)^×, of order (l−1)l^{n−1}, so an anticyclotomic character of exact order l^m (m ≥ 1) has conductor l^{m+1}, not l^m. The construction and the proofs use l^m as the conductor, so "order" is the slip; nothing else changes. The extraction silently used the conductor reading. The source says `"In particular, V_∞/V^{∗,−}_∞ and V^{∗,−}_∞ are free Λ-modules of rank 1."`; it should be `"V^∗_∞/V^{∗,−}_∞ and V^{∗,−}_∞ are free Λ-modules of rank 1."`. The quotient used in the proof of Theorem 5.8 (p.960) is V^∗_∞/V^{∗,−}_∞, which Corollary 5.6 is cited to identify with Λ; V_∞ without the star is a different module. ### Burungale–Tian, "A rank zero p-converse to a theorem of Gross–Zagier, Kolyvagin and Rubin", Annals of Mathematics 203 (2026), no. 1 (`PAPER-BURUNGALE-TIAN-26`)

- **Error** at Theorem 2.1 and footnote 3, p.4, in arXiv 2506.03465v2 (11 October 2025), the version read; the passage is the same in v1 (4 June 2025) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Theorem 2.1. Let the notation be as above. Then we have ξ(h²_Q) = ξ((h¹/z)_Q), an equality of ideals in Z_p[[G_{p∞f}]]⊗Q." Footnote 3: "The formulation in Theorem 2.1 is then identical with the one in [11, §7.2]."`; it should be `ξ((h¹/z)_Q) = ξ(h²_Q)·∏_{𝔩|f, 𝔩∤p} ξ(Z_p[[G_{p∞f}/D_𝔩]]_Q), D_𝔩 the decomposition group of 𝔩; equivalently ξ((h¹/z)_Q) = ξ(H²_Q) with H² = lim_{K'} H²(O_{K'}[1/pf], Z_p(1)), which is the module of Johnson-Leung–Kings §7.2. The printed equality holds when every prime factor of f lies above p, and after localizing at height-one primes on components ramified at every 𝔩|f with 𝔩∤p.`. z is generated by the elliptic units of the single modulus p^n f (Kato §15.5), whose projection to a character χ carries ∏_{𝔩|f,𝔩∤p}(1 − χ(𝔩)Frob_𝔩^{−1}) (Johnson-Leung–Kings Lemma 7.7); JLK accordingly pair it with H² over O_K[1/pf]. BT's h² is the class-group limit, which Kato (15.6.2) identifies at height one with H² over O_K[1/p]. By purity, 0 → H²(O[1/p]) → H²(O[1/pf]) → ⊕_{𝔩|f,𝔩∤p} Z_p[[G/D_𝔩]] → 0, and since D_𝔩 has Z_p-rank one each term is torsion but not pseudo-null. On the trivial-character component 1 − Frob_𝔩^{−1} is a non-unit even after inverting p, so the printed equality fails whenever f has a prime factor not above p, and footnote 3's "identical" is false for h². The same imprecision is in Kato's quotation of Rubin (Astérisque 295, Theorem 15.2(2)); Rubin's elliptic units include all moduli g|f. BT do not mention the parity step or the restriction. y² = x³ − x satisfies Smith's Assumption 1.1: it has full rational 2-torsion and no rational cyclic 4-isogeny. - **Misprint** at Remark 4.7, p.13, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2. The source says `"Let m = 2 and let φ : V^k_2 → A²_k be the map induced by the k-algebra morphism k[s_1, s_2] → E^k_2 = k[z]/(z³), s_1 ↦ z mod z³, s_2 ↦ 0. The map φ is a closed immersion supported at x which is ω_i-integral for i = 1, 2" (with ω_1 = ds_1 + s_1²ds_2, ω_2 = ds_1 + s_2²ds_2).`; it should be `s_1 ↦ 0, s_2 ↦ z mod z³. Then φ pulls ω_1 back to 0 and ω_2 back to z²dz, which is zero in Ω¹_{E^k_2/k} = (k[z]/(z²))dz, so φ is ω_i-integral for i = 1, 2 and the bound (4.4), m ≤ 2, is attained.`. With s_1 ↦ z and s_2 ↦ 0, both forms pull back to dz, which is nonzero in (k[z]/(z²))dz, so φ is not ω_1- or ω_2-integral and the example does not show sharpness. With the coordinates exchanged, the restrictions of ω_1 to the lines s_1 = ±s_2 are (1 ± s²)ds, nonvanishing at the origin, so (4.4) gives m ≤ 2 and the corrected jet attains it. Noted by the extraction (gap G2, item sharp-example-source), which checked the page image. - **Misprint** at §5, conventions on intersections of cycles, p.16, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2. The source says `"If Z_1 and Z_2 are algebraic cycles of dimensions r_1 and r_2, the intersection Z_1.Z_2 is an algebraic cycle of dimension 2n − r_1 − r_2 defined up to algebraic equivalence."`; it should be `Of dimension r_1 + r_2 − n.`. On an n-dimensional abelian variety, cycles of dimensions r_1 and r_2 have codimensions n − r_1 and n − r_2. Their intersection has codimension 2n − r_1 − r_2, that is, dimension r_1 + r_2 − n. The later computations, for example deg(H^{n−2}.3H.X) on p.20, use the correct count. Noted by the extraction. - **Misprint** at Proof of Lemma 5.4, p.17, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2. The source says `"… where the last equality uses tr(α) = −2(γ_1 + ... + γ_n) and (5.1)."`; it should be `tr(α) = 2(γ_1 + … + γ_n).`. P_α(t) = Q(t)² with Q monic of degree n and roots γ_i, so the coefficient of t^{2n−1} in P_α is −2Σγ_i. The paper defines tr(ψ) as the negative of that coefficient (§5.1), so tr α = 2Σγ_i. Combined with (5.1), tr α_A(C,H) = 2 deg(C.H), this gives Σγ_i = deg(C.H), which is what the arithmetic–geometric mean step uses. With the printed sign, the positive γ_i would have negative sum. Noted by the extraction (gap G3). - **Error** at Proof of Lemma 5.5(i), p.18, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2 (it affects the proof). The source says `"If (i) holds we consider the ample divisor H = X and note that Lemma 5.3 gives deg_H(X) = deg(X³)_A = cdeg(X) = c_1²(X). Thus, condition (5.4) in case (i) simplifies to p > 2c_1²(X)/3, which is implied by (5.3)." (Here (5.3) is p > c_1²(X).)`; it should be `With n = 3 and H = X, condition (5.4) reads p > (3!/3³) · c_1²(X)³ / c_1²(X) = 2c_1²(X)²/9. Lemma 5.5(i) should assume p > max(c_1²(X), 2c_1²(X)²/9). The hypothesis p > (128/9)c_1²(X)² of Theorem 9.1(i), under which the lemma is used (Lemma 9.10), implies this.`. The substitution gives 2s²/9 with s = c_1²(X), not 2s/3. For s ≥ 5, (5.3) does not imply it; for example s = 6 and p = 7 satisfy p > s but not p > 2s²/9 = 8. So the proof of case (a) under (i), which applies Lemma 5.4, is not justified by (5.3) alone. The lemma may still hold at the printed threshold, but the argument does not show it. The main theorems assume the stronger bound. Checked on the page image. - **Error** at §7.1–7.2, the series Ψ^{[m]}, Lemma 7.4 and the definitions of Log^t and Exp^t, pp.23–24, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2 (it affects the proof). The source says `"Given an integer m, let Ψ^{[m]} = (Ψ^{[m]}_1, ..., Ψ^{[m]}_n) be the power series expansion of the morphism [m] : A → A of multiplication by m in terms of t." "From [11] III.5.3 Proposition 2(i) we get Lemma 7.4. The terms of degree less than m in Ψ^{[m]}_j vanish for each j = 1, ..., n." "Log^t_j(y) = Σ_{m≥1} ((−1)^m/m) · Ψ^{[m]}_j(y), Exp^t_j(x) = Σ_{m≥1} (1/m!) · ψ^{[m]}_j(x)", with ψ^{[m]}_j the homogeneous part of degree m of Ψ^{[m]}_j.`; it should be `Take Ψ^{[m]} to be the m-th finite difference of the multiplication maps, Ψ^{[m]} = Σ_{i=0}^{m} (−1)^{m−i} C(m,i) [i], the series of Bourbaki III.5.3. Take Log^t_j(y) = Σ_{m≥1} ((−1)^{m−1}/m) Ψ^{[m]}_j(y). With these changes Lemma 7.4 is Bourbaki's statement. Lemma 7.2 still holds, as Ψ^{[m]} is an integral combination. Exp^t is the exponential, and the proofs of Lemmas 7.5–7.7 go through as written.`. As defined, Ψ^{[m]} is multiplication by m, whose linear term is m·t_j, so Lemma 7.4 fails already for the additive formal group and m = 2. Then Log^t_j has linear term Σ_m (−1)^m y_j, which does not converge coefficientwise. The printed Exp^t is also wrong in general. For the one-dimensional formal group with logarithm f(t) = t + at² + bt³, the printed Exp^t (degree-m parts of [m], weighted by 1/m!) has cubic coefficient 6a² − 4b, while the true exponential f^{−1} has 2a² − b; the finite differences give the correct 2a² − b, checked by exact computation to degree 4. The sign: with finite differences, Σ ((−1)^{m−1}/m) Ψ^{[m]} equals the logarithm f, while the printed (−1)^m/m gives −f, which is not inverse to Exp^t as Lemma 7.7(ii) asserts. The bounds used later (|b_{j,α}| ≤ ‖α‖^{[K:Q_p]}, m!c_{j,α} ∈ R) hold for the corrected series, so the main results are unaffected. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (gap G4, items formal-low-degree-source, formal-log-source and formal-exp), which proposed a different repair; the finite-difference reading is given here. - **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 9.7, p.31, in arXiv 2102.01055v2 (10 February 2021, the latest arXiv version, 39 pp.; PDF SHA-256 d7398c4b…67f7); the published article, Invent. Math. 234 (2023), 1197–1250 (54 pp., substantially revised), was not accessible and was not collated, so this finding concerns v2 (it affects the proof). The source says `"Thus, we can take v_1, v_2 ∈ ker(f) ∩ R^n which are K-linearly independent. After scaling, we can assume that v_1, v_2 ∈ R^n are primitive. If there are b_1, b_2 ∈ R^× with b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 ∈ pR^n ⊆ R^n then there are c_1, c_2 ∈ R such that b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 = ϖc_1v_1 + ϖc_2v_2 which is not possible since v_1, v_2 are K-linearly independent. Hence the images of v_1, v_2 in k^n are k-linearly independent."`; it should be `Take v_1, v_2 to be two members of an R-basis of the saturated lattice ker(f) ∩ R^n. That lattice is a direct summand of R^n of rank n − 1 ≥ 2, so any basis of it reduces to k-linearly independent vectors.`. The step from b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 ∈ ϖR^n to b_1v_1 + b_2v_2 ∈ ϖ(Rv_1 + Rv_2) assumes that Rv_1 + Rv_2 is saturated in R^n. For v_1 = e_1 and v_2 = e_1 + ϖe_2 in R³, both are primitive and K-independent, but v_2 − v_1 = ϖe_2 and their reductions coincide. The lemma itself holds by the corrected choice, so nothing later changes. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (gap G5, item primitive-proof-source). ### Česnavičius, "Purity for the Brauer group", Duke Mathematical Journal (2019) (`PAPER-CESNAVICIUS-19`)

- **Gap** at Proposition 2.2 and its proof, p.4, in arXiv 1711.06456v4 (1 December 2018, the latest version; PDF SHA-256 a62a12bb…8709); the author's copy of 27 November 2018 on his homepage has the same text; the published text, Duke Math. J. 168 (2019), 1461–1486, was not collated (it affects the proof). The source says `"Proposition 2.2. For a finite, flat map R → R′ of local rings, an open subscheme V ⊂ Spec R, and an affine, smooth R-group scheme G, if (1) Γ(Spec R, O) ≅ Γ(V, O) via pullback; and (2) every G-torsor is trivial over R; then the following pullback is injective: H¹_et(V, G) ↪ H¹_et(V_{R′}, G). (2.2.1)" Proof: "… every element of X(V) lifts to (Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′}))(V), so, by [Gir71, III.3.2.2], the map H¹_et(V, G) → H¹_et(V, Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′})) (2.2.2) is injective."`; it should be `State Proposition 2.2 for commutative G, which covers both of its uses (Proposition 2.3, with G = Res_{R′/R}(T_{R′})/T, and Corollary 2.4, with G = T). For noncommutative G, supply the missing step: for every G-torsor P over V, the twisted map (^P Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′}))(V) → (^P X)(V) must also be surjective.`. Write H = Res_{R′/R}(G_{R′}) and X = H/G. Surjectivity of H(V) → X(V) shows that the fibre of H¹(V, G) → H¹(V, H) over the trivial class is trivial. When G is commutative this is injectivity, because the map is a group homomorphism. For a noncommutative G, H¹ is only a pointed set. The fibre through the class of a torsor P is the orbit set of (^P H)(V) acting on (^P X)(V), for the twists by P (Giraud III.3.2; cf. Serre, Galois Cohomology, I.5.5, Cor. 2). The proof checks only the untwisted case. The twisted forms ^P X live only over V, so hypothesis (1) and the affineness of X give no control of their V-points. If P extends to R it is trivial by (2), so the problem is exactly the torsors on V that do not extend. Proposition 2.2 is used only in the proofs of Proposition 2.3 (G = Res_{R′/R}(T_{R′})/T) and Corollary 2.4 (G = T, an R-torus), where G is commutative, so the main theorems are unaffected. Noted by the extraction (gap G-NONABELIAN, items h1-descent and h1-abelian), which did not claim the statement false; neither is it claimed false here. Recorded as `PAPER-CESNAVICIUS-19/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Duke Mathematical Journal: Crossref record of DOI 10.1215/00127094-2018-0057, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or other update is registered; arXiv 1711.06456: v1 (17 November 2017), v2 (24 November 2017), v3 (17 January 2018), v4 (1 December 2018, the latest); the author's copy https://www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~kestutis.cesnavicius/brauer-purity.pdf (dated 27 November 2018, SHA-256 4187331e…97a1), checked 22 September 2026: Proposition 2.2 and its proof are unchanged, and no erratum is linked; web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.

### François Charles, "Birational boundedness for holomorphic symplectic varieties, Zarhin's trick for K3 surfaces, and the Tate conjecture", Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), no. 2 (`PAPER-CHARLES-16`)

- **Error** at Corollary 2.8 and its proof, pp.499–500, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"If p > 0, we can find nonnegative integers λ and t such that 0 < λ ≤ v² and |disc(NS(X))| = p^t λ |disc(NS(M_H(v)))|." Proof: "… we have a natural injection of lattices with torsion cokernel Zv ⊕ (v^⊥ ∩ N(X)) ↪ N(X). Since the discriminant of Zv ⊕ v^⊥ is v² disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X)), this implies by the same argument that |disc(N(X))| ≤ v² |disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X))|."`; it should be `λ need not be an integer. With I = [N(X) : Zv ⊕ (v^⊥ ∩ N(X))], one has |disc NS(X)| = p^t (v²/I²) |disc NS(M_H(v))| with t ≥ 0. So λ = v²/I² is a positive rational number with λ ≤ v², and I divides v² because v is primitive. For p ∤ v², for example v² = 2 and p ≥ 3 as in the applications, λ is still a p-adic unit, which is what the later arguments use.`. Take v = (1, 0, −1), the Mukai vector of ideal sheaves of length-two subschemes, so M_H(v) = X^[2]. It satisfies condition (C): it is primitive, of rank 1, v² = 2, and gcd(1, 0, −1) = 1. Then v^⊥ ∩ N(X) = NS(X) ⊕ Z(1, 0, 1) with (1, 0, 1)² = −2, so |disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X))| = 2|disc NS(X)|. The proof's first step gives |disc(v^⊥ ∩ N(X))| = p^r |disc NS(M_H(v))|. Hence |disc NS(X)| = ½ p^r |disc NS(M_H(v))|, which is not of the form p^t λ with λ a positive integer when p is odd. The proof passes from the equality disc(Zv ⊕ K) = I² disc N(X) to an integer multiplier without controlling I, here I = 2. The inequality stated for p = 0 is correct. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (items 34–35). Recorded as `PAPER-CHARLES-16/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Annals of Mathematics article page https://annals.math.princeton.edu/2016/184-2/p04 and the Crossref record of DOI 10.4007/annals.2016.184.2.4, checked 22 September 2026: no erratum or update; arXiv 1407.0592: v1 (2 July 2014) and v2 (24 August 2014, the latest); F. Charles's publication page https://www.math.ens.psl.eu/~charles/, checked 22 September 2026: it links an erratum for Invent. Math. 194 (2013) only, not for this paper; web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Error** at Proposition 2.9, p.500, as used in the proof of Theorem 2.10, p.503, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Proposition 2.9. Let d be a positive integer, and let Λ be a rank 2 positive definite sublattice of Λ_2d. There exists a positive integer N and nonzero integers a, b such that if m is any positive integer satisfying (i) m = 1[N]; (ii) m is prime to a and b, and both a and b are quadratic residues modulo m; then there exists a primitive embedding of Λ into Λ_2md." Proof: "In the setting of the proposition, the primitive embedding of Λ into Λ_2d corresponds to a tuple (V, W, γ, t)."`; it should be `Assume Λ is a primitive sublattice of Λ_2d. In the proof of Theorem 2.10, choose the lattice Λ containing v, w with v² = 2, v·w = 1 to be primitive. For example, in Λ_2d = ⟨2d⟩ ⊕ U with basis h, e, f, take the span of v = e + f and w = kh + e. It is primitive, since (x, y, z) ↦ (z, y − z) retracts it, and its Gram matrix [[2, 1], [1, 2dk²]] is positive definite.`. The proof starts from a primitive embedding Λ ⊂ Λ_2d, which the statement does not provide, and the statement fails for non-primitive Λ. In Λ_2 = ⟨2⟩ ⊕ U, the sublattice Λ generated by 2h and 2(e + f) has Gram matrix diag(8, 8). Take m = 1, which satisfies (i) and (ii) for every N, a, b. A primitive embedding of Λ into Λ_2 would give a 2-dimensional subspace of Λ_2/2Λ_2 = F_2³ on which the half-norm Q(x, y, z) = x² + yz vanishes, since Λ has all half-norms even. The nonzero zeros of Q are (0,0,1), (0,1,0) and (1,1,1), and each pairwise sum has Q = 1, so no such plane exists. Noted by the extraction (items 38–41), which also gave the primitive choice above. - **Gap** at Proof of Proposition 3.1, p.506, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2) (it affects the proof). The source says `"By Riemann-Roch, for any p and q as above, the morphism H⁰(C, 3L|_C) → 3L|_C/(3L|_C − D(p) − D(q)) is surjective. Indeed, the degree of 3L|_C − D(p) − D(q) is 3r − 4 > r = deg(ω_{C/k}); note that r is even, so that r > 1."`; it should be `Pass to 2L before this step, as the proof already allows ("up to replacing L by 2L"), so that r = L² ≥ 4 and 3r − 4 > r holds.`. 3r − 4 > r holds only for r > 2. "r is even, so that r > 1" leaves r = 2, the double-plane case, where 3r − 4 = r and the surjectivity argument as written does not apply. The proposition only claims the existence of some L′ with bounded invariants, so doubling L is allowed. Noted by the extraction (item 50), which also leaves the characteristic-two nonreduced-curve argument as an open check. The source says `"Then there exist a positive integer N and nonzero integers a, b such that there exist only finitely many polarized nonsuperspecial K3 surfaces (X, H) of degree 2md over k, where m is a positive integer satisfying (i) m = 1[N]; (ii) m is prime to a and b, and both a and b are quadratic residues modulo m; (iii) the p-adic valuation of the discriminant of NS(X_k̄) is at most t_0."`; it should be `There are only finitely many k-isomorphism classes of nonsuperspecial K3 surfaces X over k admitting a polarization H of such a degree 2md with (i)–(iii). The polarized pairs themselves need not be finite in number.`. On a fixed eligible (X, H) of degree 2m_0 d, the line bundles H^{⊗ℓ^j} have degree 2(ℓ^{2j} m_0)d. Choose a prime ℓ with a and b quadratic residues modulo ℓ and prime to N, a and b, and let j run through multiples of the order of ℓ² modulo N. Then m = ℓ^{2j} m_0 satisfies (i) and (ii), and (iii) is unchanged. This gives infinitely many pairwise non-isomorphic polarized pairs on one surface; the paper does not require polarizations to be primitive. The proof bounds NS(X_k̄) and then the surfaces. The only use, the proof of Theorem 1.3 on p.523, reads "there exist only finitely many K3 surfaces over k", so the corrected statement is what is used. Noted by the extraction (items 88–89). - **Error** at §4.1, the definition of N_{α/r}(X) and (4.1), pp.518–519, in the published version, Annals of Mathematics 184 (2016), 487–526 (publisher PDF, SHA-256 3425f269…40e2). The source says `"Let N_{α/r}(X) be the preimage of N(X) by T_{−α/r}. This is the group denoted by CH_{α/r}(X, Z) in [LMS14]. By [LMS14, Lemma 3.3.3], we have (4.1) N_{α/r}(X) = {(ar, D + aα, cω) | a, c ∈ Z, D ∈ NS(X)}."`; it should be `Define N_{α/r}(X) directly as the lattice (4.1), the twisted Mukai lattice CH_{α/r}(X, Z) of [LMS14], and drop "the preimage of N(X) by T_{−α/r}".`. With T_{−α/r}(x) = x ∪ e^{−α/r} and e^{−α/r} = (1, −α/r, α²/(2r²)), one computes T_{−α/r}(ar, D + aα, cω) = (ar, D, (c − (α·D)/r − aα²/(2r))ω). Its last coordinate is not an integer in general, for α ∈ H²(X, Z_ℓ(1)) primitive and r = ℓ^n. So the lattice (4.1) is not the preimage of N(X). Lemma 4.2, Proposition 4.3 and §4.2 compute with the explicit lattice (4.1), so the results are unaffected. Noted by the extraction (§4.1 conventions, items 93–106). (9.3) is a displayed decomposition, introduced with "for the moment we assume there is such an action, and that it gives rise to a decomposition". The centralizer is Z(O^∨) = ∏_{i odd} Sp(r_i) × ∏_{j even} O(r_j), so the so(r_j) factors that carry the points are those with j even. The preceding sentence has just shown ν_i = 0 for i odd. (5) G^∨ = E_8: (a) O^∨ = D_6: … n_v(17) = n_u(17) = 0, n_v(16) = 2 > 1 = n_v(16)."`; it should be `n_v(9) = n_u(9) = 0 in (3)(a); n_v(9) = 1 > 0 = n_u(9) in (4); n_v(16) = 2 > 1 = n_u(16) in (5)(a).`. Each item exhibits a weight i_0 where Property A fails, that is, n_u(i) = n_v(i) for i > i_0 but n_u(i_0) < n_v(i_0). The other items state exactly this with n_u on the right. As printed, "n_v(9) = 1 > 0 = n_v(9)" and "n_v(16) = 2 > 1 = n_v(16)" are self-contradictory, and "n_v(9) = n_v(9)" is vacuous. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction. The source says `"This is a free Z-module 𝓛 ⊂ V_R of rank ℓ = C(d+r, d) − n. We set L = 𝓛 ⊗_Q R as the underlying R-vector space and L^⊥ as its orthogonal complement in V_R."`; it should be `L = 𝓛 ⊗_Z R, the real span of 𝓛 in V_R (equivalently (𝓛 ⊗_Z Q) ⊗_Q R).`. - **Misprint** at Proposition 8.6, displayed formula, in arXiv 2010.00657v3 (5 September 2022; the authors' final version, source dated 14 May 2022) and in the 14 May 2022 PDF on M. Kakde's homepage, p.52. The February 2022 version on S. Dasgupta's homepage (p.53) does not have it. The published version, Annals of Mathematics 197 (2023), 289–388, could not be checked (paywalled).. The source says `"For a cusp [𝒜] ∈ C₀(𝔠, 𝔫), the normalized constant term c_𝒜(0, W_k(ψ_𝔓, 1)) equals 𝔱 · τ(ψ)/N𝔠₀^k · sgn(N(−c)) ψ(𝔠_𝒜) · L(ψ^{−1}, 1−k)/2^n · ∏_{𝔭|𝔓}(1 − ψ(𝔭)/N𝔭^k) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱}(1 − ψ(𝔩)) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱^c}(1 − N𝔩^k)."`; it should be `Delete the leading 𝔱: c_𝒜(0, W_k(ψ_𝔓, 1)) = τ(ψ)/N𝔠₀^k · sgn(N(−c)) ψ(𝔠_𝒜) · L(ψ^{−1}, 1−k)/2^n · ∏_{𝔭|𝔓}(1 − ψ(𝔭)/N𝔭^k) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱}(1 − ψ(𝔩)) · ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔱^c}(1 − N𝔩^k).`. 𝔱 is an integral ideal (the auxiliary modulus of §8.1), not a scalar, so the printed product has no meaning. The proof computes the constant term as the displayed expression with Σ_{𝔪|𝔱} μ(𝔪) ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔪^c} N𝔩^k ∏_{𝔩∈J_𝔪} ψ(𝔩) in place of the last two products, and evaluates that sum by the Möbius identity; no factor 𝔱 appears. The February 2022 version prints the formula without it (with 𝔮 for 𝔩). Checked on the page images of both author versions and in the arXiv v3 source (`\ft` at the start of the display). Noted by the extraction (item 221). - **Gap** at Proof of Theorem 1.4, p.36 (arXiv v3; published Annals version not collated) (it affects the proof). The source says `"By the Northcott property and Torelli's Theorem, there are up-to Q̄-isomophism only finitely many C_Q̄'s … such that h(ι([Jac(C_Q̄)])) < c1. By applying Raynaud's result on the Manin–Mumford Conjecture to each one of these finitely many curves separately, we obtain Theorem 1.4."`; it should be `For each of the finitely many curves C a bound for #((C − P₀) ∩ Jac(C)_tors) uniform in P₀ ∈ C(ℚ̄) is needed, because Theorem 1.4 quantifies over all P₀. For g ≥ 3 it follows from Raynaud's theorem for the surface C − C ⊆ Jac(C): its torsion lies in finitely many points and elliptic torsion cosets, and a coset t + E pulls back to a curve in C × C with no component C × {P₀}, so each P₀ meets it in boundedly many points. For g = 2, C − C = Jac(C), and a uniform Manin–Mumford for the translates C − P₀ is required, e.g. Kühne's later uniform Manin–Mumford theorem (arXiv:2101.10272), which proves Theorem 1.4 without the dependence on d.`. Raynaud's theorem gives, for each embedding C − P₀ ⊆ Jac(C), a finite set, with no control of its size as P₀ varies over the infinitely many points of C(ℚ̄). The statement is true, but the displayed step does not prove it. ### Dittmann–Pop, "Characterizing finitely generated fields by a single field axiom", Annals of Mathematics 198 (2023), no. 3 (`PAPER-DITTMANN-POP-23`)

- **Misprint** at §3, definition of Pfister forms, p.6, in arXiv 2012.01307v2 (27 April 2023, the final author version, SHA-256 f9f26f7d…2c1f); the published Annals text (198 (2023), 1203–1227) was not collated. The source says `"For an field F and a ∈ F^× set ⟨⟨a⟩⟩ = x_1² − a x_2², respectively ⟨⟨a]] := x_1² + x_1x_2 + a x_2². … If char(F) = 2, then q_a := q_{a_i,…,a_0} := ⟨⟨a_i⟩⟩ ⊗ … ⟨⟨a_1⟩⟩ ⊗ ⟨⟨a_0]]."`; it should be `In characteristic 2 the factors ⟨⟨a_j⟩⟩ (j ≥ 1) are the bilinear Pfister forms ⟨1, −a_j⟩_b of [EKM, 9.B], and q_a is their product with the quadratic form ⟨⟨a_0]]: a nonsingular quadratic form of dimension 2^{i+1}.`. With ⟨⟨a⟩⟩ read as the quadratic form x_1² − a x_2² just defined, the tensor product in characteristic 2 is not defined (quadratic forms do not multiply there), and that form is totally singular, being (x_1 + √a x_2)². The reference [EKM, 9.B] has the bilinear factors. Noted by the extraction (item pfister-two). The source says `"Since ḡ ≠ 0, there is an e-tuple ζ in the algebraic closure of κv such that ḡ(ζ) ≠ 0. Then ζ is an e-tuple of roots of unity of order prime to char(κv), and we identify ζ with its lift in the algebraic closure of κ."`; it should be `Choose ζ in the torus, with all coordinates nonzero and ḡ(ζ) ≠ 0 (possible because ḡ is a nonzero polynomial over the infinite field κv^alg); then each coordinate is a root of unity of order prime to char(κv) and has a Teichmüller-type lift.`. An element of the algebraic closure of a finite field is a root of unity only if it is nonzero, and a nonvanishing point of ḡ may have zero coordinates. Noted by the extraction (report, §5). Duke's page has the same text there (it affects the proof). The source says `"Weyl(⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ, χ) ≪_ε r^C |D|^{13/28+ε}, (6.6) where … φ is any even Hecke–Maass cusp form with Laplace eigenvalue 1/4 + r²." Proof: "Part (6.6) of Proposition 2 follows straight from Theorem 4 and Theorem 5 of [12]."`; it should be `State (6.6) for the unit vector u = φ/‖φ‖: Weyl(u, χ) ≪_ε r^C |D|^{13/28+ε}. It follows from Theorem 4 and [12, Theorem 5] together with the Rankin–Selberg size of ‖φ‖ for the normalization a(1) = 1. It is also what the proof of Theorem 2 uses, since c(φ)⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ = ⟨f, u⟩u.`. The forms φ are normalized by a(1) = 1 in (5.7), p.962, and the Shimura relation in Theorem 4 forces this too. [12, Theorem 5] (Duke 1988, p.85, read on the page image) bounds the coefficients of a spectral weight-1/2 form with eigenvalue 1/4 + t² by |λ|^A ch(πt/2)|n|^{−2/7+ε}. Here t = r/2, so Theorem 4 gives |Weyl(⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ, χ)| ≪ r^C cosh²(πr/4)|D|^{3/4−2/7+ε}. That bound has an exponential factor, which (6.6) omits. The factor cannot simply be dropped. For a(1) = 1, Rankin–Selberg unfolding gives ⟨φ, φ⟩ ≍ L(1, sym²φ)/cosh(πr), so ⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ is about e^{πr/2} times the unit vector u. As printed, (6.6) would therefore make the Weyl integrals of u exponentially small in r, which the cited results do not give. For u the two exponentials cancel: Weyl(u, χ) = ‖φ‖ Weyl(⟨φ, φ⟩^{−1}φ, χ) ≪ L(1, sym²φ)^{1/2} r^C |D|^{13/28+ε}, and L(1, sym²φ) ≪ r^ε. The coefficient bound "c(φ) ≪ |r|^{−A}" used for Theorem 2 on p.969 holds for u, so Theorem 2 stands. Noted by the extraction ("The spectral norm gate"). ### Jessica Fintzen, "Types for tame p-adic groups", Annals of Mathematics 193 (2021), no. 1 (`PAPER-FINTZEN-21`)

- **Misprint** at Types, arXiv1810.04198v2, Definition4.6, p17; second comparison facet. The source says `B(G_{n+1},k)`; it should be `Compare the competing point x' in B(G'_{n'+1},k), using its own recovered terminal Levi; prime its competing X'_i consistently.`. The competing datum has independent length and functionals. Its point need not lie in the first datum's terminal building. The maximal-facet argument requires each datum's own facet dimension. - **Gap** at Equation (2) and the paragraph after it, p.2, and Corollary 1.3, p.3; compared with Marshall 2012, §2.1, equation (4), in arXiv 2201.11190v2 (15 February 2024, SHA-256 d71e9d3f…2614), the version the extraction read; the published Annals text (200 (2024), 123–152) was not collated (it affects the proof). The source says `"… the Eichler–Shimura isomorphism summarized as dim_C H_c^{r1+r2}(Y(K_f), W_k) = 2^{r1} dim_C S_k(K_f) (2) in [Mar12]." "This space corresponds to the first compactly supported cohomology …" Corollary 1.3: "dim_C H^n_c(Y(K_f), W_k) ∼_{K_f} k" for n = 1, 2.`; it should be `In [Mar12] H_c denotes cuspidal cohomology (inside ordinary cohomology), which is what (2) describes. For compactly supported cohomology, Corollary 1.3 needs a boundary estimate: for c cusps and n = 1, 2, |dim H^n_c − dim H^n| ≤ 3c (a cusp-torus Koszul computation with dimensions (1,2,1)), with the trivial coefficient system treated separately.`. Fu attributes (2) to Marshall but reads Marshall's H_c as compactly supported cohomology. The difference is a boundary term bounded in terms of the number of cusps, so the two-sided growth in Corollary 1.3 survives, but the paper does not supply that step. Converted from the extraction's older-form finding S1 ("source-convention mismatch; boundary proof required"): finding: Marshall defines H_c as cuspidal cohomology inside ordinary cohomology, whereas Fu describes it as compactly supported. Marshall also states the displayed dimension identity for nontrivial coefficient systems. Resolution recorded there: Use the cuspidal injection for the upper bound. For the full compact-support interpretation retain an explicit boundary long-exact-sequence calculation and Poincare-Lefschetz comparison, treating the trivial coefficient separately. The two-sided Bianchi result uses the specified base-change lower-bound level class. The continuation gives a cusp-torus Koszul argument with dimensions(1,2,1) and resulting |dim H_c^i−dim H^i|<=3c for i=1,2 and c cusps; its geometric and chain-comparison imports remain explicit. Items: cuspidal-support-dictionary, bianchi-boundary-estimate, bianchi-cohomological-sharpness. The source says `"We let D_r(G, K) := completion of D(G, K) with respect to the norm ‖·‖_r. As a K-Banach space, D_r(G, K) = {Σ_α λ_α b^α | λ_α ∈ K, sup_{α∈N^d} |λ_α| r^{|α|} < ∞}. (16)"`; it should be `The completion consists of the series with |λ_α| r^{|α|} → 0. The space with sup < ∞ is the larger Banach space (Schneider–Teitelbaum's D_{<r}-type space).`. The Fréchet algebra D(G,K) = lim_r D_r(G,K) is the same with either description, so the later arguments are unaffected; the proof of Theorem 5.7 cites (16) for a density statement that holds for the completion. Converted from the extraction's older-form finding S2 ("explicit definition correction"): finding: The norm-completion description of D_r needs weighted coefficients tending to zero. Equation (16) gives only a boundedness condition; Schneider-Teitelbaum distinguishes the larger bounded space D_<r. Resolution recorded there: Keep D_r and D_<r separate. For example r=p^(-1/p) and coefficients c_(pj)=p^(-j) give a bounded weighted sequence of value 1 which does not tend to zero. Not every integral Casimir parameter is induced by a Q_p-valued torus character: for p = 3 and λ = 1, the condition a(a+2)/2 = 1 forces (a+1)² = 3, impossible in Q_3 since v_3(3) is odd. No certification of this proof interior is claimed here. An explicit integral PBW division with bounded remainder/quotient operators now supplies a route for the U_r central normal form. For r=p^(-2/3), lambda=p^(-1), the Casimir has norm at most p^(2/3)<|lambda|, so Delta-lambda is a unit and the central quotient is zero. Resolution recorded there: Record the source's unrestricted statement, the explicit zero-quotient theorem and the integral-parameter target separately. Do not infer separatedness from a zero associated graded. This correction leaves the integral-parameter main theorem untouched. Items: radius-central-domain, large-central-character-zero-quotient. ### Hacon–Witaszek, "On the relative minimal model program for fourfolds in positive and mixed characteristic", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2023) (`PAPER-HACON-WITASZEK-23`)

- **Error** at Lemma 2.2, second paragraph, p.5 of the published version (arXiv v2 Lemma 2.2, p.6). The source says `"Let P ∈ S be any codimension 1 point on S and m the Cartier index of K_X at P. Then on a neighbourhood of P ∈ X, mD is Cartier for any divisor D, and S is normal, and (K_X + S)|_S = K_S + (1 − 1/m)P."`; it should be `m should be the Cartier index of K_X + S at P, the log index. For the plt surface germ obtained by localising at P, this equals the order of the local class group, and the conclusions then hold.`. Counterexample. Take X = Spec k[x,y,z]/(xy − z²), the A1 surface singularity with char k > 5, S = V(x, z), Δ = S and P the vertex. Then X is Q-factorial, (X, S) is plt and hence dlt, and P is a codimension-one point of the curve S. The minimal resolution (charts (x,z,y) = (u,uv,uv²) and (st²,st,s)) is crepant, so K_X is Cartier and m = 1. But div(x) = 2S and the ideal (x, z) is not principal at P, so S is not Cartier. Since f*S = S′ + E/2, adjunction gives (K_X + S)|_S = K_S + ½P, not K_S. With m = index(K_X + S) = 2 every clause holds. For a threefold example, multiply by A¹. The only other use is Lemma 4.2 (p.16), which needs only that the different of a plt pair with standard coefficients has standard coefficients; that is unaffected. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (gap-index, item adjunction-index-diagnostic). Recorded as `PAPER-HACON-WITASZEK-23/E1`; looked for an existing correction in: Crossref metadata for 10.1017/fmp.2023.6: no update, correction or erratum relation, and no work that updates this DOI (queried 2026-09-22); Forum of Mathematics, Pi article page (open access, CC BY 4.0): no correction notice located; arXiv 2009.02631 v1 (2020-09-06) and v2 (2021-08-16, the latest version); v2 has the same wording at every locator below.
- **Error** at Lemma 2.16, p.13 of the published version (arXiv v2 Lemma 2.16, p.14) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Fix m ∈ N and ε > 0. Consider all ε-lc excellent two-dimensional pairs (S, B) with the minimal log resolution having at most m distinct discrepancies. Then the set of all these discrepancies for such surfaces is finite."`; it should be `Also fix a finite set I ⊂ [0, 1) and require the coefficients of B to lie in I. Then, over the minimal resolution, the exceptional discrepancies take finitely many values. This is what the proof of Proposition 2.15 needs, since the boundaries B_n of a flip sequence all have the coefficients of B.`. Counterexample. On the A1 surface S = {xy = z²} with C = V(x, z) and B = bC, 0 < b < 1/2, the minimal resolution is one (−2)-curve E, and K_{S′} + bC′ + (b/2)E = f*(K_S + bC). The pair is ½-lc for every such b (log discrepancies 1 − b/2 and 1 − b on an snc model with coefficients < 1/2), and there is one exceptional discrepancy, −b/2, so m = 1. That value varies infinitely. The proof fails because the right-hand sides B_{S′}·C_i of its linear system contain f^{-1}_*B·C_i, which varies with B. With coefficients in I the right-hand sides lie in a finite set, since f^{-1}_*B·C_i = 2r_i + a_iC_i² − Σ_{j≠i}(1 − a_j)C_j·C_i < 2r_i ≤ 8. Proposition 2.15 is unaffected. Noted by the extraction (gap-surface, item surface-finiteness-diagnostic); the corrected statement is proved in fixed-boundary-surface by this continuation. - **Misprint** at Proof of Lemma 2.16, p.13 of the published version (arXiv v2 p.15). The source says `"a_iC_i² = (K_{S′} + B_{S′} + a_iC_i)·C_i ≥ −2r_i, and so −c_i ≤ 2/a_i is bounded."`; it should be `c_i ≤ 2/a_i ≤ 2/ε.`. The proof writes C_i² = −r_ic_i, so c_i > 0 and −c_i ≤ 2/a_i is vacuous. The displayed inequality −a_ir_ic_i ≥ −2r_i gives c_i ≤ 2/a_i, which is the bound the proof then uses ("and so −C_i² is bounded"). Checked on the page image. - **Misprint** at Proof of Proposition 2.15, definition of the difficulty d⁺_α, p.12 of the published version (arXiv v2, same passage). The source says `"d⁺_α(Y, B_Y) = Σ_{b_i ≤ 0} W⁺_α(b_i)ρ(B̃_i) + Σ_v w⁺_α(a_v) − Σ_{C̃_{i,j}} W⁺_α(b_i)"`; it should be `The first sum is over b_i ≥ 0, which is AHK07 Definition 2.3's index a(B_i) ≤ 0 with a(B_i) = −b_i.`. W⁺_α(b) = Σ_{k≥1} w⁺_α(k(1 − b)), and for b ≤ 0 every argument k(1 − b) ≥ 1 > α, so W⁺_α(b) = 0. The printed first sum is therefore identically zero, and the difficulty would lose its boundary term, which is exactly what drops when a flip changes ρ. AHK07 Definition 2.3 indexes the sum by a(B_j) ≤ 0. Checked on the page image and against AHK07 (arXiv math/0605137v2). Noted by the extraction (gap-difficulty). - **Error** at Lemma 5.5, p.19, in the author final version (33 pp., revised 9 December 2021, SHA-256 d95100eb…546f; identical to arXiv 1904.06512v2) (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Let Γ be a profinite group and 0 → A → B → C → 0 be a short exact sequence of discrete Γ-modules whose underlying sequence of abelian groups is split. … Let [γ] ∈ H¹(Γ, Hom(C,A)) be the class of this torsor"`; it should be `Assume C finitely generated (for example finite, as in the only application, C = P^{r,s}/Z). Otherwise Hom(C,A) with the discrete topology need not carry a continuous class.`. Counterexample: Γ = ∏ C₂ (countably many factors), A = ⊕ F₂a_i and C = ⊕ F₂c_i with trivial action, B = A ⊕ C with g·c_i = c_i + g_i a_i. B is a discrete module and the sequence splits as groups, but γ(g)(c_i) = g_i a_i has kernel {1}, which is not open, so γ is not continuous into the discrete module Hom(C,A). Found by the extraction (item 139). The source says `"Lemma 4.7. If 2 ≤ n ≤ 6, the outer exponent of U¹ is equal to p. Proof. As the abelianization of U¹ has exponent p when n ≥ 2, the outer exponent of U¹ is divisible by p."`; it should be `For 2 ≤ n ≤ 6 the powering action of (Z/d)^× on conjugacy classes of U¹ factors through (Z/p)^×; hence the outer exponent is p for p odd and 1 for p = 2.`. Definition 3.2 takes the least divisor e of the exponent through which the action factors. For p = 2, (Z/2)^× = (Z/1)^× is trivial and the proof shows the action is trivial (n ≤ 3 by Lemma 4.6; 4 ≤ n ≤ 6 because M^{−1} is conjugate to M), so e = 1, not 2; the divisibility step holds only for odd p. The lemma is used only to make the cyclotomic character χ : G → (Z/e)^× trivial when μ_p ⊂ k and to choose L ⊇ μ_e, which is unchanged for e = 1 since μ₂ ⊂ k; the main theorems are unaffected. Noted by the extraction (items 42, 65). The source says `"We are thus reduced to checking that W(k_v) ≠ ∅ for any place v of k … which is exactly what Proposition 5.3 provides when applied to the restriction of α to each Γ_{k_v}", while p.19 says: "Here and below, by a local field, we mean a complete discretely valued field with finite residue field."`; it should be `Treat the archimedean places separately. For v complex, Γ_{k_v} is trivial. For v real and p odd, U/P^{r,s} is a p-group, so the restriction of α to Γ_{k_v} ≅ C₂ is trivial. For v real and p = 2, the proof of Proposition 5.3 goes through: the split case is group theory, and in the non-split case H¹(R, Hom(P/Z, μ₂)) × H¹(R, P/Z) → H²(R, μ₂) is perfect (Tate duality over R), so cup product with [γ] is onto.`. The global argument needs local solvability at every place, but Proposition 5.3 is stated only for non-archimedean fields; the real places with p = 2 need an argument, which is short. Noted by the extraction (items 91, 117). ### He, "Cocenters of p-adic groups, I: Newton decomposition", Forum of Mathematics, Pi (2018) (`PAPER-HE-18`)

- **Error** at §2.1(a) p.9; Corollary9 p.12; §5.2 p.20; Theorem23 pp.24–25 (it affects a stated result). The source says `Each θ-twisted conjugacy class of W̃ intersects only finitely many Wa cosets.`; it should be `For the finite-minimal-element and defined-X_ν compactness claims, add a finite-fibre condition on Ω→Ω_θ (ordinary θ=id is safe), or replace the argument and compact core. Ordinary finite θ-orbits do not control twisted Ω-orbits.`. For G=G_m(F), θ inversion, W̃=Ω=Z, Wa=1, all lengths zero and θ has order2. Twisted conjugation sends m to m+2k; π records parity and Newton0. Thus every parity class contains infinitely many minimal elements and X_ν is the noncompact union of all corresponding valuation cosets. This does not refute the Newton partition, finiteness of conjugacy-CLASS fibres, or existence of a different compact core. In Theorem23 finite ordinary θ-saturation of Ω labels likewise need not be twisted-conjugacy stable. ### Kisin–Madapusi Pera–Shin, "Honda-Tate theory for Shimura varieties", Duke Mathematical Journal (2022) (`PAPER-KISIN-MADAPUSIPERA-SHIN-22`)

- **Misprint** at (1.1.2.2), p.6, in the author PDF https://math.berkeley.edu/~swshin/HT.pdf (41 pp., created 27 January 2021, SHA-256 fd22990b…52db), the latest version found; the published text, Duke Math. J. 171 (2022), 1559–1614, was not collated; the older author version newton2.pdf (18 August 2018) on M. Kisin's page has the same text. The source says `"There exists c ∈ G(L) and an integer r ∈ Z_{>0} such that rν_b factors through a cocharacter G_{m,L} → G_L, that c(rν_b)c^{−1} is defined over the fixed field of σ^r on L, and that c b σ(b) σ²(b) ··· σ^r(b) σ^r(c)^{−1} = c(rν_b)(p)c^{−1}."`; it should be `c b σ(b) ··· σ^{r−1}(b) σ^r(c)^{−1} = c(rν_b)(p)c^{−1}.`. The r-th power of bσ is b σ(b) ··· σ^{r−1}(b) σ^r, with r factors. As printed there are r + 1: for G = G_m and b = p the left side is p^{r+1} but the right side is p^r. Kottwitz [Kot85, 4.3], which is cited, and the paper's own (1.1.4.1) on p.7, "b σ(b) σ²(b) ··· σ^{r−1}(b) = (rν_b)(p)", have r factors. Checked on the page image. Noted by the extraction (source discrepancy 1, item N09). W. Shin's errata page https://math.berkeley.edu/~swshin/errata.pdf, checked 22 September 2026: it lists errata for three other papers, none for this one; M. Kisin's copy https://people.math.harvard.edu/~kisin/dvifiles/newton2.pdf (34 pp., created 18 August 2018, SHA-256 f3f2b164…2f70): an earlier version, not a correction; web search for an erratum or correction, 22 September 2026: none found.
- **Error** at Corollary 1.1.15 and its proof, pp.11–12, in the author PDF https://math.berkeley.edu/~swshin/HT.pdf (41 pp., created 27 January 2021, SHA-256 fd22990b…52db), the latest version found; the published text, Duke Math. J. 171 (2022), 1559–1614, was not collated; the older author version newton2.pdf (18 August 2018) on M. Kisin's page has the same text (it affects a stated result). The source says `"Corollary 1.1.15. Let G be an arbitrary connected reductive group over Q_p with a Q_p-rational Levi subgroup M. Let μ: G_m → M be a minuscule cocharacter and [b_M] ∈ B(M)_b such that ([b_M], {μ}) is G-admissible. Then there exists w ∈ W(G, M) := N_G(M)/M such that ([b_M], {w·μ}) is M-admissible." Proof: "(1.1.13) implies that there exists w* ∈ W(G*, M*) such that ([b_{M*}], {w*·μ*}) is M*-admissible."`; it should be `Replace N_G(M)/M by the absolute Weyl group W(G, T) of a maximal torus T ⊂ M with μ ∈ X_*(T), as in Proposition 1.1.13. Equivalently: some G(Q̄_p)-conjugate μ′ of μ factoring through T makes ([b_M], {μ′}) M-admissible.`. The printed statement fails already for split G. Take G = GL_3, M = GL_1 × GL_2 (blocks of sizes 1 and 2), μ = (0,1,0) and b_M = diag(p,1,1), which is basic in M. Then ([b_M], {μ}) is G-admissible: its Newton point (1,0,0) equals the dominant μ̄, and both have κ_G = 1. But κ_M(b_M) = (1,0), while κ_M(μ) = (0,1). Since the blocks have different sizes, N_G(M) = M, so W(G, M) is trivial and no w in it helps. The permutation of the first two coordinates, in W(G, T) = S_3, does. The proof transports to the quasi-split inner form and applies Proposition 1.1.13. That proposition gives w* in the absolute Weyl group W(G*, T*), not in N_{G*}(M*)/M*, so the proof establishes the corrected statement. The only use is in the proof of Corollary 1.1.17, p.13, for G not necessarily quasi-split. It cites "Lemma 1.1.13" (the quasi-split Proposition 1.1.13) in the form w ∈ W(G, T). The corrected Corollary 1.1.15 is exactly what that step needs, so the later results stand. Checked on the page images. Noted by the extraction (source discrepancy 2, items N29 and N32), which also verified the Weyl-group computation by machine. - **Gap** at Proof of Lemma 2.2.8, p.28, in the author PDF https://math.berkeley.edu/~swshin/HT.pdf (41 pp., created 27 January 2021, SHA-256 fd22990b…52db), the latest version found; the published text, Duke Math. J. 171 (2022), 1559–1614, was not collated (it affects the proof). The source says `"Let G′ = G × T, equipped with the symplectic representation V′ = V ⊕ V. Let X′ = X × {h_T}. Then we have (G′, X′) ↪ (G_{V′}, H_{V′}). … Restricting the action of I_{s′_0} to A_T induces a map of Q-groups I_{s′_0} → T, and we consider the composite I_{s′_0} → T → G^ab. By Corollary 2.1.9, I_{s′_0} → I_{s_0} is surjective, so the map I_{s′_0} → G^ab factors through I_{s_0}, as this is true over Q_ℓ for any ℓ ≠ p."`; it should be `Take G′ = G ×_{G^ab} T, the pairs (g, t) with the same image in G^ab. This lies in GSp(V ⊕ V, ψ ⊕ ψ), and X′ = X × {h_T} still maps into it because h^ab is constant on X. The kernel of G′ → G is T ∩ G^der, which dies in G^ab, so the composite I_{s′_0} → T → G^ab does factor through I_{s_0}. The rest of the proof then goes through.`. Read literally, the construction fails at two points. First, G × T acting on V ⊕ V does not preserve ψ ⊕ ψ up to a scalar when the similitude factors of g and t differ, so it is not a subgroup of G_{V′}. Second, the claimed factorization fails. The CM automorphisms (1, t), t ∈ T, act trivially on A_{s_0} and by t on A_T, and they preserve the tensors cutting out G × T. So 1 × T lies in I_{s′_0}, and also in the kernel of I_{s′_0} → I_{s_0}. But under I_{s′_0} → T → G^ab it maps onto G^ab, as T is a maximal torus of G. So the map does not factor through I_{s_0} unless G^ab = 1. The same happens over Q_ℓ, so the justification "as this is true over Q_ℓ" fails too. Surjectivity of I_{s′_0} → I_{s_0} alone cannot give a factorization. What is needed is that the kernel maps trivially, and the fibre product above provides this. Lemma 2.2.8 is used in the proof of Proposition 2.2.2 (p.28). Noted by the extraction (source discrepancy 7, item T23) as a descent gate; the analysis and the fibre-product repair are given here.
